1c1
< Standard LLM benchmarks evaluate each model under a single prompt template and random seed, implicitly treating these design choices as negligible variance sources. We show this assumption is wrong and that it inflates item requirements by an order of magnitude. Applying Generalizability theory---a psychometric framework for multi-facet reliability analysis---to a factorial design crossing seven facets across eight open-weight models and five benchmarks (2.3 million binary records), we find that the dominant evaluation variance source is not the prompt, the seed, or numerical precision---it is the model$\times$item interaction (30--36\%), meaning models disagree on which specific items are difficult rather than on overall ability. Multi-facet designs that vary prompts and seeds absorb this interaction noise, achieving reliable measurement with roughly 100 items where single-condition protocols require over 1,000. We further discover that the variance structure is format-dependent: item-related factors dominate multiple-choice benchmarks but disperse in free-form tasks. These findings establish design-dependent reproducibility budgets for LLM evaluation and suggest that reliability standards should be calibrated per benchmark format. We release an open-source G-theory pipeline and D-study budget calculator\footnote{Code and data are available at \url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/nlp_variance_gtheory-11E1/}.}.
---

1	# Generalizability Theory for LLM Evaluation Variance Decomposition
2	
3	Code and data for applying Generalizability Theory (G-theory) to decompose variance in LLM benchmark evaluations, using Henderson Method I to estimate variance components across evaluation facets (prompt template, temperature, random seed, item ordering).
4	
5	## Requirements
6	
7	- Python 3.10+
8	- GPU with CUDA support (for vLLM inference)
9	
10	```bash
11	pip install -r requirements.txt
12	```
13	
14	## Quick Start
15	
16	**1. Prepare data and design matrix**
17	
18	```bash
19	python -m src.data.sample_mmlu --output data/mmlu_items.json
20	python -m src.data.few_shot --output data/few_shot_examples.json
21	python -m src.design.generate_design --output design_matrix.csv
22	```
23	
24	**2. Run inference**
25	
26	```bash
27	python -m src.inference.run_experiment \
28	    --design design_matrix.csv \
29	    --items data/mmlu_items.json \
30	    --few-shot data/few_shot_examples.json \
31	    --output results/all_results.jsonl \
32	    --gpu-id 0
33	```
34	
35	**3. G-study: variance decomposition**
36	
37	```bash
38	python -m src.analysis.variance_decomposition \
39	    --input results/all_results.jsonl \
40	    --output results/variance_components.json \
41	    --n-bootstrap 1000
42	```
43	
44	**4. D-study: budget optimization**
45	
46	```bash
47	python -m src.analysis.d_study \
48	    --input results/variance_components.json \
49	    --output results/d_study_results.json
50	```
51	
52	The D-study calculator (`src/analysis/d_study.py`) computes generalizability coefficients for alternative designs and finds minimum replications needed to reach target reliability thresholds (G >= 0.90, 0.95, 0.99).
53	
54	## Repository Structure
55	
56	```
57	src/
58	  data/           Data loading and sampling
59	  design/         Experimental design matrix generation
60	  inference/      vLLM-based model inference
61	  analysis/       G-study, D-study, bootstrap CI, visualization
62	scripts/          Experimen
…[truncated 211 chars]

_source line 2077 · 2026-05-26T12:27:24.272859+00:00_

1	LLM benchmarks are the primary tool for comparing language models, guiding deployment, funding, and ranking decisions. Every evaluation involves design choices (prompt template, random seed, numerical precision, answer ordering) typically fixed to single values and assumed inconsequential. Growing evidence challenges this assumption: prompt formatting can shift accuracy by 76 percentage points, and answer ordering can flip model rankings. Yet each study examines one factor in isolation, leaving unanswered which sources dominate when all vary simultaneously and how many items suffice. We address this with Generalizability theory, a psychometric framework for multi-source variance decomposition. In a factorial experiment crossing seven facets across eight models and five benchmarks (2.3M records), we find that item difficulty is the largest variance component (34--38\% in multiple-choice), and the model$\times$item interaction is the second largest (30--36\% in MC, 15--17\% in free-form), exceeding every manipulated design factor by an order of magnitude---models disagree on which items are difficult, not in overall ability. A 3-prompt $\times$ 3-seed protocol absorbs this interaction, reducing items from 552 to 178 ($G \geq 0.80$). We observe a format-associated pattern ($n = 5$, exploratory): item-related variance concentrates in multiple-choice (68--72\%) but disperses in free-form tasks (31--41\%). We release an open-source G-theory toolkit\footnote{Code and data are available at \url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/nlp_variance_gtheory-11E1/}.}.
2

  "content": "# Reproducibility Budgets for Open-Weight LLM Evaluation: A Generalizability Theory Study of 7–9B Instruction-Tuned Models\n\nCode and data for applying Generalizability Theory (G-theory) to decompose variance in LLM benchmark evaluations. We use Henderson Method I to estimate variance components across seven evaluation facets (prompt template, random seed, numerical precision, sampling temperature, answer ordering, model, item) in a factorial experiment crossing eight open-weight 7–9B instruction-tuned models and five benchmarks (MMLU, ARC-Challenge, HellaSwag, GSM8K, MATH; 2.3M records).\n\n**Key findings:**\n- Item difficulty is the largest variance component (34–38% in multiple-choice)\n- Model×item interaction is the second largest (30–36% in MC, 15–17% in free-form), exceeding all manipulated design factors by an order of magnitude\n- A 3-prompt × 3-seed protocol achieves G ≥ 0.80 with 178 items versus 552 under single-condition protocols (3.1× reduction)\n- Item-related variance concentrates in multiple-choice (68–72%) but disperses in free-form tasks (31–41%)\n\n## Requirements\n\n- Python 3.10+\n- GPU with CUDA support (for vLLM inference)\n\n```bash\npip install -r requirements.txt\n```\n\n## Quick Start\n\n**1. Prepare data and design matrix**\n\n```bash\npython -m src.data.sample_mmlu --output data/mmlu_items.json\npython -m src.data.few_shot --output data/few_shot_examples.json\npython -m src.design.generate_design --output design_matrix.csv\n```\n\n**2. G-study: variance decomposition**\n\n```bash\npython -m src.analysis.variance_decomposition \\\n    --input results/all_results.jsonl \\\n    --output results/variance_components.json \\\n    --n-bootstrap 1000\n```\n\n**4. D-study: budget optimization**\n\n```bash\npython -m src.analysis.d_study \\\n    --input results/variance_components.json \\\n    --output results/d_study_results.json\n```\n\nThe D-study calculator (`src/analysis/d_study.py`) computes generalizability coefficients for alternative designs and finds minimum replications needed to reach target reliability thresholds (G ≥ 0.80, 0.90, 0.95).\n\n## Repository Structure\n\n```\nsrc/\n  data/           Data loading and sampling\n  design/         Experimental design matrix generation\n  inference/      vLLM-based model inference\n  analysis/       G-study, D-study, bootstrap CI, visualization\nscripts/          Experiment scripts for reproducing paper results\ndata/             Benchmark items and few-shot examples (JSON)\npaper/            LaTeX source\ntests/            Unit tests\n```\n\n## License\n\nMIT\n"
}

_source line 2131 · 2026-05-26T12:30:20.582460+00:00_

Here are the verification results for all 6 papers:

---

## 1. madaan2024quantifying — "Quantifying Variance in Evaluation Benchmarks"

**CORRECT authors (arXiv):** Lovish Madaan, Aaditya K. Singh, Rylan Schaeffer, Andrew Poulton, Sanmi Koyejo, Pontus Stenetorp, Sharan Narang, Dieuwke Hupkes

**CURRENT bib:** "Madaan, Lovish and Bhatia, Samarth and Tulio Ribeiro, Marco and Roth, Dan"

**Verdict: WRONG — completely fabricated author list (except Madaan).** The bib has 4 authors, the real paper has 8. Only "Lovish Madaan" is correct. "Bhatia, Samarth", "Tulio Ribeiro, Marco", and "Roth, Dan" are not authors of this paper at all.

**Fix:** Replace with `Madaan, Lovish and Singh, Aaditya K. and Schaeffer, Rylan and Poulton, Andrew and Koyejo, Sanmi and Stenetorp, Pontus and Narang, Sharan and Hupkes, Dieuwke`

---

## 2. hua2025flaw — "Flaw or Artifact? Rethinking Prompt Sensitivity in Evaluating LLMs"

**CORRECT authors (ACL Anthology):** Andong Hua, Kenan Tang, Chenhe Gu, Jindong Gu, Eric Wong, Yao Qin

**CURRENT bib:** "Hua, Angie and Tang, Kevin and Gu, Chaoqun and Gu, Jing and Wong, Edmund and Qin, Yilin"

**Verdict: WRONG — all 6 first names are fabricated.** The family names are roughly correct, but every single given name is wrong (Angie→Andong, Kevin→Kenan, Chaoqun→Chenhe, Jing→Jindong, Edmund→Eric, Yilin→Yao).

**Fix:** Replace with `Hua, Andong and Tang, Kenan and Gu, Chenhe and Gu, Jindong and Wong, Eric and Qin, Yao`

---

## 3. zhou2026irt — "Lost in Benchmarks? Rethinking Large Language Model Benchmarking with Item Response Theory"
